### Task 5: The scattnlay comparison and the benchmarks

**Files:**
- Create: `benchmarks/compare_scattnlay.py`, `scatrs/benches/layered.rs`
- Modify: `benchmarks/pyproject.toml` (`scattnlay==2.4`) and `uv.lock` (`uv lock`; this also brings the stale lock up to date), `scatrs/Cargo.toml` (the bench)
- Generate: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-14-arm64-scattnlay.json`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the script and the bench** (diff below).
- [ ] **Step 2: Run them.**

Run: `uv run --locked --directory benchmarks python compare_scattnlay.py` and `cargo bench -p scatrs --bench layered`
Expected: the results table of "Measured results". scattnlay prints "nmax value was changed due to unexpected error" for tiny particles; those are its failures.

- [ ] **Step 3: Commit:** `bench(layered): the scattnlay comparison and kernel benchmarks` (`d452611`). The prototype's commit missed a clippy `type_complexity` error, fixed in `7ed9393`; the diff below includes the fix. Check clippy's output, not only its exit status.

````diff
diff --git a/benchmarks/compare_scattnlay.py b/benchmarks/compare_scattnlay.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..c96fc61
--- /dev/null
+++ b/benchmarks/compare_scattnlay.py
@@ -0,0 +1,360 @@
+"""Accuracy and speed of scatrs's layered spheres against scattnlay, both measured against Arb.
+
+Accuracy: every case of the Arb reference suite (reference/data/layered.jsonl) inside scatrs's
+validated domain, for scatrs, for scattnlay (Peña & Pal 2009, the recursion scatrs builds on,
+in double precision), and for direct interface matching in double precision (the textbook
+method of Aden & Kerker 1951, which Bohren & Huffman's BHCOAT implements): the coefficients aₙ
+and bₙ (max over the stored orders of |error| / max |a|), Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs, Q_back and g
+(relative errors; Q_back relative to max(Q_back, Q_sca), g to max(|g|, 10⁻³)), and S₁, S₂
+(relative to max(|S|, S_rms)), each code with its own number of terms.
+
+Speed: the efficiencies of coated spheres (black carbon in sulfate, core radius 0.5 of the
+outer) at outer size parameters 1, 10, 100 and 1000, one thread, best of three; scatrs for the
+values alone and with every derivative (in the derivatives' domain, x_L ≤ 100), and a sphere of
+the shell's index at the same size for comparison.
+
+Conventions: scattnlay takes the same m (Im m ≥ 0 absorbing) and returns the same aₙ, bₙ, S₁
+and S₂ (e^(−iωt)), so no conjugation is needed.
+
+Usage: uv run --directory benchmarks python compare_scattnlay.py [--quick]
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import datetime
+import importlib.metadata
+import json
+import os
+import platform
+import time
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import numpy as np
+
+os.environ.setdefault("RAYON_NUM_THREADS", "1")
+
+import scattnlay
+
+from scatrs import layered, mie
+
+DATA = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "reference" / "data" / "layered.jsonl"
+CODES = ("scatrs", "scattnlay", "direct")
+OUTPUTS = ("coefficients", "q_ext", "q_sca", "q_abs", "q_back", "g", "amplitudes")
+
+
+def value(r: dict, name: str) -> np.ndarray:
+    """The reference values of output `name`: hi + lo (double-double accuracy is plenty)."""
+    o = r["outputs"][name]
+    return np.array(o["hi"]) + np.array(o["lo"])
+
+
+def cases() -> list[tuple[dict, dict]]:
+    """(coefficients, series) record pairs."""
+    records = [json.loads(line) for line in DATA.read_text().splitlines()]
+    series = {
+        r["case"].removesuffix(",series"): r for r in records if r["case"].endswith(",series")
+    }
+    return [
+        (r, series[r["case"].removesuffix(",coefficients")])
+        for r in records
+        if r["case"].endswith(",coefficients")
+    ]
+
+
+def inputs(r: dict) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
+    i = r["inputs"]
+    return np.array(i["x"]), np.array(i["m_re"]) + 1j * np.array(i["m_im"])
+
+
+def inside(x: np.ndarray, m: np.ndarray) -> bool:
+    try:
+        layered.efficiencies(x, m)
+    except Exception:
+        return False
+    return True
+
+
+def direct(x: np.ndarray, m: np.ndarray, n: int, mus: np.ndarray):
+    """aₙ, bₙ, the efficiencies and S₁, S₂ by direct interface matching in complex128, with
+    ψ and ξ from upward recurrences (the textbook method)."""
+
+    def riccati(z: complex) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
+        psi = np.zeros(n + 1, complex)
+        xi = np.zeros(n + 1, complex)
+        psi[0], xi[0] = np.sin(z), -1j * np.exp(1j * z)
+        psi_m1, xi_m1 = np.cos(z), np.exp(1j * z)  # ψ₋₁ = cos z, ξ₋₁ = e^(iz)
+        for k in range(1, n + 1):
+            prev_p = psi[k - 2] if k >= 2 else psi_m1
+            prev_x = xi[k - 2] if k >= 2 else xi_m1
+            psi[k] = (2 * k - 1) / z * psi[k - 1] - prev_p
+            xi[k] = (2 * k - 1) / z * xi[k - 1] - prev_x
+        return psi, xi
+
+    def deriv(f: np.ndarray, z: complex, prev0: complex) -> np.ndarray:
+        d = np.zeros_like(f)
+        d[0] = prev0
+        k = np.arange(1, n + 1)
+        d[1:] = f[:-1] - k * f[1:] / z
+        return d
+
+    with np.errstate(all="ignore"):
+        out = []
+        for kind in "ab":
+            z0 = m[0] * x[0]
+            p0, _ = riccati(z0)
+            h = deriv(p0, z0, np.cos(z0)) / p0
+            for j in range(1, len(x)):
+                z1, z2 = m[j] * x[j - 1], m[j] * x[j]
+                p1, x1 = riccati(z1)
+                p2, x2 = riccati(z2)
+                dp1, dx1 = deriv(p1, z1, np.cos(z1)), deriv(x1, z1, np.exp(1j * z1))
+                dp2, dx2 = deriv(p2, z2, np.cos(z2)), deriv(x2, z2, np.exp(1j * z2))
+                t = (m[j] / m[j - 1] if kind == "a" else m[j - 1] / m[j]) * h
+                c = (t * p1 - dp1) / (dx1 - t * x1)
+                h = (dp2 + c * dx2) / (p2 + c * x2)
+            px, xx = riccati(complex(x[-1]))
+            nn = np.arange(1, n + 1)
+            big = (h[1:] / m[-1] if kind == "a" else m[-1] * h[1:]) + nn / x[-1]
+            out.append((big * px[1:] - px[:-1]) / (big * xx[1:] - xx[:-1]))
+        a, b = out
+        return a, b, series(a, b, x[-1], mus)
+
+
+def series(a: np.ndarray, b: np.ndarray, x: float, mus: np.ndarray) -> dict:
+    """Q_ext, Q_sca, Q_abs = Q_ext − Q_sca, Q_back, g, S₁, S₂ from the coefficients."""
+    n = np.arange(1, len(a) + 1)
+    w = 2 * n + 1
+    q_ext = 2 / x**2 * np.sum(w * (a + b).real)
+    q_sca = 2 / x**2 * np.sum(w * (abs(a) ** 2 + abs(b) ** 2))
+    q_back = abs(np.sum(w * (-1.0) ** n * (a - b))) ** 2 / x**2
+    g = (
+        4
+        / (x**2 * q_sca)
+        * (
+            np.sum(
+                n[:-1]
+                * (n[:-1] + 2)
+                / (n[:-1] + 1)
+                * (a[:-1] * a[1:].conj() + b[:-1] * b[1:].conj()).real
+            )
+            + np.sum(w / (n * (n + 1)) * (a * b.conj()).real)
+        )
+    )
+    s1, s2 = [], []
+    for mu in mus:
+        pi = np.zeros(len(a) + 1)
+        pi[1] = 1.0
+        for k in range(2, len(a) + 1):
+            pi[k] = ((2 * k - 1) * mu * pi[k - 1] - k * pi[k - 2]) / (k - 1)
+        tau = n * mu * pi[1:] - (n + 1) * pi[:-1]
+        c = w / (n * (n + 1))
+        s1.append(np.sum(c * (a * pi[1:] + b * tau)))
+        s2.append(np.sum(c * (a * tau + b * pi[1:])))
+    return {
+        "q_ext": q_ext,
+        "q_sca": q_sca,
+        "q_abs": q_ext - q_sca,
+        "q_back": q_back,
+        "g": g,
+        "s1": np.array(s1),
+        "s2": np.array(s2),
+    }
+
+
+def run(code: str, x: np.ndarray, m: np.ndarray, mus: np.ndarray, n_ref: int):
+    """(a, b, series) of one code."""
+    if code == "scatrs":
+        c = layered.coefficients(x, m)
+        q = layered.efficiencies(x, m)
+        s = layered.amplitudes(x, m, mus)
+        out = {k: getattr(q, k) for k in ("q_ext", "q_sca", "q_abs", "q_back", "g")}
+        return c.a, c.b, out | {"s1": s.s1, "s2": s.s2}
+    if code == "scattnlay":
+        _, a, b = scattnlay.scattcoeffs(x, m)
+        theta = np.arccos(np.clip(mus, -1, 1))
+        _, q_ext, q_sca, q_abs, q_back, _, g, _, s1, s2 = scattnlay.scattnlay(x, m, theta)
+        # scattnlay's Q_bk is 4π dσ/dΩ / (π r²) at 180°, as scatrs's Q_back.
+        out = {"q_ext": q_ext, "q_sca": q_sca, "q_abs": q_abs, "q_back": q_back, "g": g}
+        return a, b, out | {"s1": s1, "s2": s2}
+    n = int(np.ceil(x[-1] + 4.05 * x[-1] ** (1 / 3) + 2))
+    a, b, out = direct(x, m, n, mus)
+    return a, b, out
+
+
+def errors(r: dict, s: dict, got) -> dict[str, float]:
+    a, b, out = got
+    orders = np.array(r["inputs"]["orders"])
+    ref_a = value(r, "a_re") + 1j * value(r, "a_im")
+    ref_b = value(r, "b_re") + 1j * value(r, "b_im")
+    scale = max(abs(ref_a).max(), abs(ref_b).max())
+
+    def at(c: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+        c = np.ravel(c)
+        return np.array([c[k - 1] if k <= len(c) else 0.0 for k in orders])
+
+    e = {
+        "coefficients": max(abs(at(a) - ref_a).max(), abs(at(b) - ref_b).max()) / scale,
+    }
+    q = {k: value(s, k)[0] for k in ("q_ext", "q_sca", "q_abs", "q_back", "g")}
+    e["q_ext"] = abs(float(np.ravel(out["q_ext"])[0]) - q["q_ext"]) / q["q_ext"]
+    e["q_sca"] = abs(float(np.ravel(out["q_sca"])[0]) - q["q_sca"]) / q["q_sca"]
+    e["q_abs"] = (
+        abs(float(np.ravel(out["q_abs"])[0]) - q["q_abs"]) / q["q_abs"] if q["q_abs"] > 0 else 0.0
+    )
+    e["q_back"] = abs(float(np.ravel(out["q_back"])[0]) - q["q_back"]) / max(
+        q["q_back"], q["q_sca"]
+    )
+    e["g"] = abs(float(np.ravel(out["g"])[0]) - q["g"]) / max(abs(q["g"]), 1e-3)
+    s_rms = r["inputs"]["x"][-1] * np.sqrt(q["q_sca"]) / 2
+    worst = 0.0
+    for name in ("s1", "s2"):
+        ref = value(s, f"{name}_re") + 1j * value(s, f"{name}_im")
+        got_s = np.ravel(out[name])
+        worst = max(worst, (abs(got_s - ref) / np.maximum(abs(ref), s_rms)).max())
+    e["amplitudes"] = worst
+    return {k: float(v) for k, v in e.items()}
+
+
+def accuracy(quick: bool) -> dict:
+    pairs = cases()
+    if quick:
+        pairs = pairs[::20]
+    per_code = {c: {o: [] for o in OUTPUTS} for c in CODES}
+    worst = {c: {o: (0.0, "") for o in OUTPUTS} for c in CODES}
+    used = 0
+    for r, s in pairs:
+        x, m = inputs(r)
+        if not inside(x, m):
+            continue
+        used += 1
+        mus = np.array(s["inputs"]["mu"])
+        for code in CODES:
+            try:
+                e = errors(r, s, run(code, x, m, mus, s["inputs"]["n_ref"]))
+            except Exception:
+                e = dict.fromkeys(OUTPUTS, float("inf"))
+            for o in OUTPUTS:
+                v = e[o] if np.isfinite(e[o]) else float("inf")
+                per_code[code][o].append(v)
+                if not v <= worst[code][o][0]:
+                    worst[code][o] = (v, r["case"].removesuffix(",coefficients"))
+    summary = {}
+    for code in CODES:
+        summary[code] = {}
+        for o in OUTPUTS:
+            v = np.array(per_code[code][o])
+            finite = v[np.isfinite(v)]
+            summary[code][o] = {
+                "median": float(np.median(finite)) if finite.size else None,
+                "max_finite": float(finite.max()) if finite.size else None,
+                "worst_case": worst[code][o][1],
+                "above_1e-10": int(np.sum(~(v <= 1e-10))),
+                "above_1e-6": int(np.sum(~(v <= 1e-6))),
+                "failed": int(np.sum(~np.isfinite(v))),
+            }
+    return {"cases": used, "summary": summary}
+
+
+def best_of(f, repeats: int = 3) -> float:
+    best = float("inf")
+    for _ in range(repeats):
+        t = time.perf_counter()
+        f()
+        best = min(best, time.perf_counter() - t)
+    return best
+
+
+def speed(quick: bool) -> list[dict]:
+    rows = []
+    m = np.array([1.95 + 0.79j, 1.43 + 1e-8j])
+    for x_l in (1.0, 10.0, 100.0) if quick else (1.0, 10.0, 100.0, 1000.0):
+        count = max(1, int(2000 / x_l))
+        x = np.tile([0.5 * x_l, x_l], (count, 1))
+        mm = np.tile(m, (count, 1))
+        t_scatrs = best_of(lambda x=x, mm=mm: layered.efficiencies(x, mm)) / count
+        t_deriv = (
+            best_of(lambda x=x, mm=mm: layered.efficiencies(x, mm, derivatives=True)) / count
+            if x_l <= 100
+            else float("nan")
+        )
+        t_snl = (
+            best_of(
+                lambda x=x, mm=mm: [
+                    scattnlay.scattnlay(xi, mi) for xi, mi in zip(x, mm, strict=True)
+                ]
+            )
+            / count
+        )
+        xs = np.full(count, x_l)
+        t_sphere = best_of(lambda xs=xs: mie.efficiencies(xs, m[1])) / count
+        rows.append(
+            {
+                "x": x_l,
+                "sphere_us": t_sphere * 1e6,
+                "scatrs_us": t_scatrs * 1e6,
+                "scatrs_derivatives_us": t_deriv * 1e6,
+                "scattnlay_us": t_snl * 1e6,
+            }
+        )
+        print(rows[-1], flush=True)
+    return rows
+
+
+def markdown(report: dict) -> str:
+    lines = [
+        f"{report['machine']}; scatrs {report['versions']['scatrs']}, scattnlay "
+        f"{report['versions']['scattnlay']}, {report['date']}; {report['accuracy']['cases']} "
+        "cases of the reference suite inside the validated domain.",
+        "",
+        "| output | scatrs: largest error | scattnlay: cases > 1e-10, > 1e-6, failed |"
+        " direct: cases > 1e-10, > 1e-6, failed |",
+        "|---|---|---|---|",
+    ]
+    s = report["accuracy"]["summary"]
+
+    def counts(c: dict) -> str:
+        return f"{c['above_1e-10']}, {c['above_1e-6']}, {c['failed']}"
+
+    for o in OUTPUTS:
+        lines.append(
+            f"| {o} | {s['scatrs'][o]['max_finite']:.1e} | {counts(s['scattnlay'][o])} | "
+            f"{counts(s['direct'][o])} |"
+        )
+    lines += [
+        "",
+        "| x_L | scatrs (µs) | with derivatives (µs) | scattnlay (µs) | a sphere of x_L (µs) |",
+        "|---|---|---|---|---|",
+    ]
+    for r in report["speed"]:
+        lines.append(
+            f"| {r['x']:g} | {r['scatrs_us']:.1f} | {r['scatrs_derivatives_us']:.1f} | "
+            f"{r['scattnlay_us']:.1f} | {r['sphere_us']:.1f} |"
+        )
+    return "\n".join(lines)
+
+
+def main() -> None:
+    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
+    p.add_argument("--quick", action="store_true", help="fewer cases, to check the script")
+    args = p.parse_args()
+    report = {
+        "date": datetime.date.today().isoformat(),
+        "machine": platform.platform(),
+        "versions": {
+            "scatrs": importlib.metadata.version("scatrs"),
+            "scattnlay": importlib.metadata.version("scattnlay"),
+            "python": platform.python_version(),
+        },
+        "accuracy": accuracy(args.quick),
+        "speed": speed(args.quick),
+    }
+    stamp = f"{report['date']}-{platform.machine()}-scattnlay"
+    path = Path(__file__).parent / "results" / f"{stamp}{'-quick' if args.quick else ''}.json"
+    path.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2) + "\n")
+    print(f"\nwrote {path}\n")
+    print(markdown(report))
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    main()

diff --git a/scatrs/benches/layered.rs b/scatrs/benches/layered.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..58cdcf9
--- /dev/null
+++ b/scatrs/benches/layered.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,74 @@
+//! Wall-clock benchmarks of the layered-sphere kernel (criterion): the efficiencies of two-,
+//! three- and ten-layer particles across outer size parameters, and with every derivative (in
+//! the derivatives' domain), against a sphere of the same outer size parameter. The theory
+//! chapter's "Cost" section records the results.
+//!
+//! ```text
+//! cargo bench -p scatrs --bench layered
+//! ```
+
+use std::hint::black_box;
+
+use criterion::{BenchmarkId, Criterion, criterion_group, criterion_main};
+use num_complex::Complex;
+use scatrs::layered::Layered;
+use scatrs::mie::{Mie, MieOptions};
+
+const SIZES: [f64; 5] = [0.1, 1.0, 10.0, 100.0, 1000.0];
+
+/// (name, radius fractions, indices).
+fn particles() -> Vec<(&'static str, Vec<f64>, Vec<Complex<f64>>)> {
+    let c = Complex::new;
+    vec![
+        (
+            "2-layer",
+            vec![0.5, 1.0],
+            vec![c(1.95, 0.79), c(1.43, 1e-8)],
+        ),
+        (
+            "3-layer",
+            vec![0.3, 0.7, 1.0],
+            vec![c(1.5, 0.1), c(2.5, 1.0), c(1.33, 0.0)],
+        ),
+        (
+            "10-layer",
+            (1..=10).map(|l| f64::from(l) / 10.0).collect(),
+            (1..=10)
+                .map(|l| c(1.6 - 0.02 * f64::from(l), 1e-4))
+                .collect(),
+        ),
+    ]
+}
+
+fn layered(c: &mut Criterion) {
+    let opts = MieOptions::new();
+    let mut group = c.benchmark_group("layered");
+    for x_l in SIZES {
+        let mut mie = Mie::<f64>::new();
+        let m = Complex::new(1.43, 1e-8);
+        group.bench_with_input(BenchmarkId::new("sphere", x_l), &x_l, |b, &x| {
+            b.iter(|| mie.compute(black_box(x), m, &opts).unwrap().efficiencies())
+        });
+        for (name, fractions, m) in particles() {
+            let x: Vec<f64> = fractions.iter().map(|f| f * x_l).collect();
+            let mut lay = Layered::<f64>::new();
+            group.bench_with_input(BenchmarkId::new(name, x_l), &x, |b, x| {
+                b.iter(|| lay.compute(black_box(x), &m, &opts).unwrap().efficiencies())
+            });
+            if x_l <= 100.0 {
+                let id = BenchmarkId::new(format!("{name}+derivatives"), x_l);
+                group.bench_with_input(id, &x, |b, x| {
+                    b.iter(|| {
+                        lay.compute_with_derivatives(black_box(x), &m, &opts)
+                            .unwrap()
+                            .efficiencies_jacobian()
+                    })
+                });
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    group.finish();
+}
+
+criterion_group!(benches, layered);
+criterion_main!(benches);

+

diff --git a/docs/theory/layered.md b/docs/theory/layered.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..d0a7ea1
--- /dev/null
+++ b/docs/theory/layered.md
@@ -0,0 +1,218 @@
+# Layered spheres
+
+<!-- synced with scatrs/src/layered/kernel.rs and scatrs/src/mie/kernel.rs -->
+
+A layered sphere is a core with any number of concentric shells. Its scattering has the same
+form as a homogeneous sphere's: coefficients $a_n$ and $b_n$, and from them every output of
+{doc}`mie` (efficiencies, amplitudes, phase matrix and Greek coefficients), computed by the
+same code. Only the coefficients differ. This chapter describes how scatrs computes them, why
+each choice was made, and how accurate the results are. A coated sphere is the case of two
+layers.
+
+## Geometry and conventions
+
+The layers are numbered from the core, $l = 0, \dots, L - 1$. Layer $l$ lies between the radii
+$r_{l-1}$ and $r_l$ (with $r_{-1} = 0$) and has the refractive index $m_l$ relative to the
+medium. Its outer surface has the size parameter $x_l = 2\pi r_l/\lambda$, so
+$0 < x_0 \le x_1 \le \dots \le x_{L-1}$; a layer of zero thickness is allowed. The outer size
+parameter $x_L = x_{L-1}$ normalizes the efficiencies and the phase matrix, and sets the
+number of terms. The conventions are those of {doc}`conventions`: time dependence
+$e^{-i\omega t}$, $\mathrm{Im}\,m \ge 0$ for absorption, and the Riccati–Bessel functions
+$\psi_n$, $\chi_n$ and $\xi_n = \psi_n - i\chi_n$.
+
+In layer $l$ the field of order $n$ is a combination of $\psi_n(m_l k r)$ and a second
+solution. Matching the tangential fields at each interface carries the information of the
+inner layers outward. At the outer surface it becomes two effective logarithmic derivatives,
+$H_a$ and $H_b$, and the coefficients are the homogeneous sphere's,
+
+$$
+a_n = \frac{(H_a/m_{L-1} + n/x_L)\,\psi_n(x_L) - \psi_{n-1}(x_L)}
+           {(H_a/m_{L-1} + n/x_L)\,\xi_n(x_L) - \xi_{n-1}(x_L)},
+\qquad
+b_n = \frac{(m_{L-1} H_b + n/x_L)\,\psi_n(x_L) - \psi_{n-1}(x_L)}
+           {(m_{L-1} H_b + n/x_L)\,\xi_n(x_L) - \xi_{n-1}(x_L)},
+$$
+
+with $H_a$ and $H_b$ in place of the sphere's $D_n(mx)$. A single layer is a sphere, and
+scatrs then calls the sphere's code itself, so that its results are bitwise those of
+{mod}`scatrs.mie`.
+
+## The recursion
+
+With $D_n = \psi_n'/\psi_n$, a second solution $\zeta_n$ with $D^\zeta_n = \zeta_n'/\zeta_n$ and
+Wronskian $W = \psi_n\zeta_n' - \psi_n'\zeta_n$, and for layer $l$ the arguments
+$z_1 = m_l x_{l-1}$ and $z_2 = m_l x_l$, the effective logarithmic derivative at the outer
+surface of layer $l$ follows from the one at its inner surface (Yang 2003; Peña & Pal 2009).
+Starting from the core's $H^{(0)} = D_n(m_0 x_0)$,
+
+$$
+G_1 = m_l H^{(l-1)} - m_{l-1} D_n(z_1), \qquad G_2 = m_l H^{(l-1)} - m_{l-1} D^\zeta_n(z_1)
+$$
+
+for $H_a$ (with $m_l$ and $m_{l-1}$ exchanged for $H_b$), and with
+$Q_n = [\psi_n/\zeta_n](z_1) \,/\, [\psi_n/\zeta_n](z_2)$,
+
+$$
+H^{(l)} = \frac{G_2 D_n(z_2) - Q_n G_1 D^\zeta_n(z_2)}{G_2 - Q_n G_1}.
+$$
+
+For $\zeta = \xi$ this is Yang's formula. Written this way it is not usable near the real zeros
+of $\psi_n(z_2)$, where $Q_n$ and $D_n(z_2)$ have poles but $H^{(l)}$ does not. scatrs uses two
+equivalent forms instead. With $\Pi_n = \psi_n\zeta_n$ and $P_n = Q_n\Pi_n(z_2)$,
+
+$$
+H^{(l)} = D^\zeta_n(z_2) - \frac{W G_2}{\Pi_n(z_2) G_2 - P_n G_1}
+        = D_n(z_2) + \delta, \qquad
+\delta = -\frac{W P_n G_1}{\Pi_n(z_2)\,\bigl(\Pi_n(z_2) G_2 - P_n G_1\bigr)}.
+$$
+
+The first form has no pole where $\psi_n(z_2)$ vanishes. The second separates the inner layers'
+contribution $\delta$ from the layer's own $D_n(z_2)$. That separation matters at the outer
+surface, where the homogeneous sphere's numerators are free of cancellation only in terms of
+$D_n$ (below). scatrs takes the second form at every order unless $D_n(z_2)$ and $\delta$
+cancel, by more than a factor of 4, which happens only near the poles of $D_n(z_2)$, and the
+first form there. The two forms were checked against a direct solution of the interface
+conditions in 60-digit arithmetic, to $10^{-40}$.
+
+The auxiliary functions come from recurrences in $n$ that stay finite:
+
+- $D_n$, through $u_n = \psi_n/\psi_{n-1}$, from the sphere's contracting downward recurrence,
+  for every argument;
+- $w_n = \zeta_n/\zeta_{n-1}$ from the upward recurrence of $\zeta$,
+  $w_{n+1} = (2n + 1)/z - 1/w_n$, which is stable for $\mathrm{Im}\,z \ge 0$, and
+  $D^\zeta_n = (n + 1)/z - w_{n+1}$;
+- $\Pi_n = \Pi_{n-1} u_n w_n$ and $P_n = P_{n-1}\,[u_n(z_1)/w_n(z_1)]\,w_n(z_2)^2$, ratios
+  that neither overflow nor underflow. $P_n$ falls off like $(x_{l-1}/x_l)^{2n+1}$ at high
+  orders, and like $e^{-2\,\mathrm{Im}\,m_l (x_l - x_{l-1})}$ through an absorbing layer: the
+  layers inside a thick absorber become invisible, as they should.
+
+The starting values $\Pi_0$ and $P_0$ involve $1 - e^{2iz}$, which loses digits for small $z$
+unless it is computed as $-\operatorname{expm1}(2iz)$; scatrs has a complex `expm1` for this.
+
+## The second solution
+
+The formulas hold for any second solution $\zeta$, and the choice matters for absorption.
+
+- $\zeta = \xi$ ($W = i$) makes the decay through an absorbing layer explicit: $|P_n| \le
+  |\Pi_n|\,e^{-2\,\mathrm{Im}(z_2 - z_1)}$. With $\zeta = \chi$ that decay would appear only
+  as a difference of nearly equal numbers.
+- $\zeta = \chi$ ($W = -1$) is real for a real argument, as $\psi$ is. Every quantity of a layer
+  with a real index is then real, and a weak absorption shows up as imaginary parts of
+  relative accuracy. With $\xi$, whose values are complex even for a real argument, the
+  absorption is a small part of large complex numbers, and their rounding buries it: the
+  relative error of $Q_\text{abs}$ grew like $2^{-52}/\mathrm{Im}\,m$, to $10^{-5}$ at
+  $\mathrm{Im}\,m = 10^{-12}$.
+
+scatrs takes $\chi$ for a layer that is optically thin, $\mathrm{Im}\,m_l\,x_l < \tfrac12$, and
+$\xi$ otherwise. On a grid of weakly absorbing particles the error of $Q_\text{abs}$ dropped to
+rounding level, from $10^{-13}$ to $10^{-5}$ before, and an absorbing core in a clear shell
+went from $7\cdot10^{-9}$ to $2\cdot10^{-16}$.
+
+## Cancellation-free numerators
+
+For small spheres the sphere's numerators would cancel catastrophically: $D_n(mx)/m$ and
+$D_n(x)$ are both about $(n + 1)/x$, and their difference is far smaller. The sphere's code
+removes the cancelling terms exactly ({doc}`mie`). A layered sphere has two more places where
+the same terms meet, and scatrs removes them there too.
+
+- **At the outer surface.** With $H = D_n(z_2) + \delta$, the numerators are the sphere's plus
+  $\delta/m$ (or $m\delta$), so the sphere's cancellation-free forms carry over. Where the
+  first form of $H$ is used, near poles of $D_n(z_2)$, nothing cancels. In the orders below
+  $x_L$, $\delta\,\psi_n(x_L)$ is added to the outer layer's own numerator rather than to
+  $D_n$ first, since $\delta$ can be far smaller than $D_n$. That own numerator is exactly
+  0 for an outer layer of index 1 (an air shell), as it is in exact arithmetic.
+- **In $G_1$.** $m_l H^{(l-1)}$ and $m_{l-1} D_n(z_1)$ are both about $(n + 1)/x_{l-1}$. With
+  $D_n = (n + 1)/z - u_{n+1}$ and $H^{(l-1)} = D_n(m_{l-1}x_{l-1}) + \delta^{(l-1)}$,
+  $$
+  G_1 = \frac{n + 1}{x_{l-1}}\Bigl(\frac{m_l}{m_{l-1}} - \frac{m_{l-1}}{m_l}\Bigr)
+        - m_l u_{n+1}(m_{l-1}x_{l-1}) + m_{l-1} u_{n+1}(m_l x_{l-1}) + m_l\delta^{(l-1)}
+  $$
+  for $H_a$; for $H_b$ the first term is exactly 0. Computed directly, $G_1$ of $b_n$ lost
+  about four digits for a core of $x_0 = 10^{-9}$.
+
+Layers with the same index then give $G_1 = 0$ exactly and $\delta = 0$, so their results are
+bitwise those of the homogeneous sphere of the outer radius. The tests check this.
+
+## Absorption
+
+The sphere's identity $\mathrm{Re}\,a_n - |a_n|^2 = -\mathrm{Im}\,A/|\Delta_a|^2$ holds for any
+complex $A$, so $Q_\text{abs}$ keeps its per-order form with $A = H_a/m_{L-1} + n/x_L$
+(likewise for $b_n$), without the cancellation of $Q_\text{ext} - Q_\text{sca}$, and
+$Q_\text{ext} = Q_\text{sca} + Q_\text{abs}$. A particle with real indices in every layer
+absorbs nothing; scatrs sets its $Q_\text{abs}$ to exactly 0, as for a real sphere.
+
+## Derivatives
+
+The derivatives with respect to each layer's $x_l$, $\mathrm{Re}\,m_l$ and $\mathrm{Im}\,m_l$
+come from dual numbers ({doc}`scalar-types`) carried through the same code. The coefficients
+are holomorphic in each $m_l$, so seeding $x_l$ and $\mathrm{Re}\,m_l$ is enough: the
+derivative with respect to $\mathrm{Re}\,m_l$ is the complex derivative $d/dm_l$, and the
+one with respect to $\mathrm{Im}\,m_l$ is $i\,d/dm_l$. The absorption sum is not holomorphic;
+its derivative with respect to $\mathrm{Im}\,m_l$ follows from the coefficients',
+
+$$
+\frac{\partial}{\partial\,\mathrm{Im}\,m_l}\bigl(\mathrm{Re}\,a_n - |a_n|^2\bigr)
+  = 2\,\mathrm{Im}\bigl(\bar a_n a_n'\bigr) - \mathrm{Im}\,a_n',
+$$
+
+with $a_n' = da_n/dm_l$, likewise for $b_n$. Because $\mathrm{Im}\,m$ is never seeded, the values
+take the same paths as without derivatives and are bitwise the same: a real index keeps the
+cheaper real recurrence, and a lossless particle keeps $Q_\text{abs} = 0$. For up to four
+layers one pass carries all $2L$ derivatives; above, one pass per layer carries two. One layer
+uses the sphere's closed forms.
+
+The derivatives lose accuracy faster than the values as the particle grows. The dual numbers
+differentiate every intermediate quantity, and for thin lossless shells some of those are far
+more sensitive than the result. Their validated domain therefore ends at $x_L = 100$
+({doc}`/validation/accuracy`). Derivatives in closed form, by the chain rule through the
+recursion, would avoid this; they are planned.
+
+## Truncation
+
+The series is truncated at the sphere's $N = \lceil x_L + 6x_L^{1/3} + 3\rceil$ terms, for the
+outer size parameter. The reference values sum to $\lceil x_L + 18x_L^{1/3} + 32\rceil$ terms,
+so the accuracy maps measure the truncation error too, including for high-index cores whose
+$|m_0| x_0$ exceeds $x_L$.
+
+## The accuracy model
+
+The accuracy is declared as for spheres ({doc}`mie`): every output is within
+$C\,(\kappa' + \mathrm{cond})\,2^{-52}$ of the exact value. Here cond sums the sensitivities
+to every layer's parameters. $\kappa'$ takes the largest index and the largest near-unity factor
+$|m_l|/|m_l - 1|$ of any layer whose index is not exactly 1:
+
+$$
+\kappa = \bigl(1 + \max_l \max(1, |m_l|)\, x_L\bigr)\,\max\Bigl(1, \max_{l:\,m_l \ne 1} \frac{|m_l|}{|m_l - 1|}\Bigr).
+$$
+
+The derivatives of the coefficients and the amplitudes take $\kappa$ itself, near-unity factor
+included, since they share the algorithm's losses near $m = 1$; a sphere's closed forms do
+not. The constants $C$ were measured on two accuracy maps and at the Arb references, each at
+least four times the worst case found, and differ from the sphere's where the layered kernel
+needs more ({rust}`scatrs::layered::ValidatedDomain::constant`). The validated domain:
+
+- $10^{-6} \le x_L \le 10^4$, and $|m_l|\,x_L \le 3\cdot10^4$ for every layer. Above this,
+  the narrow resonances of thin lossless shells of high index cost the coefficients up to
+  $10^9$ ulps;
+- every layer's index in the sphere's domain ($0 < \mathrm{Re}\,m \le 10$,
+  $0 \le \mathrm{Im}\,m \le 10$, $|m| \ge 0.1$), and at most 16 layers;
+- the derivatives up to $x_L = 100$.
+
+## Reference values
+
+The Arb reference values ({doc}`reference-suite`) do not use this algorithm. The generator
+computes $\psi_n$ and $\xi_n$ of every argument themselves, in ball arithmetic, and matches the
+fields at each interface directly, a form that would lose digits in double precision but is
+exact in Arb, where the working precision grows until every value is enclosed tightly. The
+references therefore check the recursion and its reformulations, not only their arithmetic.
+
+## Cost
+
+Each layer adds downward recurrences for $D_n$ at its two arguments, and the upward
+recurrences of $\Pi_n$, $w_n$ and $P_n$. Measured on one thread of an Apple M4 Pro
+(`cargo bench -p scatrs --bench layered`), the efficiencies of a coated sphere cost about 2.5
+to 2.7 times those of a homogeneous sphere of the same outer size: 1.7 µs at $x_L = 1$, 11 µs at
+100 and 94 µs at 1000. A three-layer particle costs 3.3 to 4 times a sphere, a ten-layer one 13
+to 16 times. Every derivative of a two-layer particle costs 4.6 to 7.4 times the values,
+of a three-layer one 5.6 to 8.9 times, and of a ten-layer one (one pass per layer) 25 to 32
+times.

+(layered-accuracy)=
+## Layered spheres
+
+<!-- synced with scatrs/src/layered/domain.rs rustdoc -->
+
+The outputs of {mod}`scatrs.layered` follow the same model ({doc}`/theory/layered`), with the
+condition numbers summed over every layer's parameters and their own constants. Inside their
+validated domain ($10^{-6} \le x_L \le 10^4$ with $|m_l|\,x_L \le 3\cdot10^4$, every index in
+the sphere's domain, at most 16 layers; the derivatives up to $x_L = 100$), four tests enforce
+them:
+
+- `scatrs/tests/layered_reference.rs` compares every output with the Arb reference values,
+  computed without scatrs's algorithm by matching the fields at each interface directly
+  ({doc}`/theory/reference-suite`): 362 particles in the domain, from $x_L = 10^{-6}$ to
+  $10^4$, including thin and thick shells, absorbing cores in clear shells, metallic shells,
+  near-unity and high-contrast indices, air shells, and up to ten layers; and the derivatives
+  for 8 particles. The 8 reference particles outside the domain must be refused.
+- `scatrs/tests/layered_accuracy_map.rs` compares the double-precision results with
+  double-double on 101 outer size parameters from $10^{-6}$ to $10^4$ against 51 particles:
+  12 pairs of core and shell indices at core radius fractions $10^{-3}$, 0.3, 0.9 and 0.999,
+  and three- and ten-layer particles.
+- `scatrs/tests/layered_greek_accuracy_map.rs` does the same for the phase matrix and the
+  Greek coefficients, on 46 outer size parameters up to $10^3$ against 21 particles.
+- `scatrs/tests/layered.rs` checks the identities that need no reference: one layer, and
+  layers of one index, are bitwise the homogeneous sphere; a shell of zero thickness changes
+  nothing; an air shell gives the bare core; the Rayleigh limit; and the derivatives against
+  central differences.
+
+The worst $C$ of each map against its declared constant, and the worst $C$ over all particles
+at each size (empty above $x_L = 100$ for the derivatives):
+
+```{code-cell} python
+def worst_layered(name):
+    """Print the worst C of each output of a layered map; check the margin of 4."""
+    data = json.loads(Path("data", name).read_text())
+    print(f"{len(data['x'])} outer size parameters × {len(data['particles'])} particles")
+    print(f"{'output':<26}{'worst C':>10}{'declared':>10}")
+    for output, rows in data["c"].items():
+        # null: outside the (derivatives') domain.
+        c = np.array(rows, dtype=float)
+        worst = np.nanmax(c)
+        print(f"{output:<26}{worst:10.2f}{data['declared'][output]:10.0f}")
+        assert 4 * worst <= data["declared"][output], f"{output}: margin below 4"
+    return data
+
+
+layered_map = worst_layered("layered-accuracy-map.json")
+layered_greek = worst_layered("layered-greek-accuracy-map.json")
+```
+
+```{code-cell} python
+fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(11, 4), sharey=True, layout="constrained")
+for ax, data, title in [
+    (axes[0], layered_map, "coefficients, efficiencies, amplitudes"),
+    (axes[1], layered_greek, "phase matrix, Greek coefficients"),
+]:
+    x = np.array(data["x"])
+    for output, rows in data["c"].items():
+        c = np.array(rows, dtype=float)
+        with np.errstate(all="ignore"):
+            worst = np.nanmax(np.where(np.isnan(c), -np.inf, c), axis=1)
+        worst[~np.isfinite(worst)] = np.nan
+        ax.loglog(x, worst, lw=1, label=output)
+    ax.set(xlabel="outer size parameter $x_L$", title=title)
+axes[0].set_ylabel("worst $C$ over the particles")
+for ax in axes:
+    ax.legend(fontsize=7)
+plt.show()
+```
+
+The values use at most a quarter of their constants anywhere. The derivatives grow with the
+particle: for thin lossless shells (a real index of 10, or an air shell around a large core)
+the amplitude derivatives use about a fifth of their constant at $x_L = 50$, and would exceed it
+by up to 6 times at $x_L \sim 10^3$, which is why their domain ends at 100.
+
 (ensemble-accuracy)=
 ## Ensembles of spheres

--- a/docs/validation/external.md

 assert np.allclose(q.q_ext, q.q_sca, rtol=1e-13, atol=0)
 ```
 
+## Layered spheres
+
+A layered sphere has its own checks from physics, which do not depend on any code.
+
+**An air shell changes nothing.** A shell of the medium's own index ($m = 1$) around a core is
+not there: the coefficients are the bare core's. scatrs computes them from the shell's
+functions at both radii, so the check exercises the whole recursion.
+
+```{code-cell} python
+from scatrs import layered
+
+for x_core, m_core in [(0.5, 1.5 + 0.01j), (12.0, 1.95 + 0.79j), (80.0, 1.33)]:
+    core = mie.coefficients(x_core, m_core)
+    n = int(core.n_terms)
+    coated = layered.coefficients([x_core, 1.6 * x_core], [m_core, 1.0], n_max=n,
+                                  allow_unvalidated=True)
+    err = max(abs(coated.a[:n] - core.a).max(), abs(coated.b[:n] - core.b).max())
+    scale = max(abs(core.a).max(), abs(core.b).max())
+    print(f"x_core = {x_core:g}, m = {m_core}: relative difference {err / scale:.1e}")
+    assert err <= 1e-12 * scale
+```
+
+**Layers of one index are one sphere.** Splitting a sphere into shells of its own index
+gives the homogeneous sphere of the outer radius, exactly:
+
+```{code-cell} python
+q = layered.efficiencies([2.0, 5.0, 9.0], [1.5 + 0.01j] * 3)
+q0 = mie.efficiencies(9.0, 1.5 + 0.01j)
+assert q.q_ext == q0.q_ext and q.q_abs == q0.q_abs and q.g == q0.g
+```
+
+**The Rayleigh limit.** A coated sphere much smaller than the wavelength scatters as a
+dipole of the electrostatic polarizability of a coated sphere (Bohren & Huffman 1983,
+chapter 5): with $\varepsilon = m^2$ of the core (1) and shell (2) and the core volume fraction
+$f = (x_1/x_2)^3$, $a_1 \to -\tfrac{2i}{3}x^3\,\tilde\alpha$ with
+
+$$\tilde\alpha = \frac{(\varepsilon_2 - 1)(\varepsilon_1 + 2\varepsilon_2) + f(2\varepsilon_2 + 1)(\varepsilon_1 - \varepsilon_2)}
+                      {(\varepsilon_2 + 2)(\varepsilon_1 + 2\varepsilon_2) + 2f(\varepsilon_2 - 1)(\varepsilon_1 - \varepsilon_2)},$$
+
+with corrections of order $x^2$:
+
+```{code-cell} python
+x_out = 1e-4
+for m1, m2 in [(1.95 + 0.79j, 1.43 + 0.01j), (1.33, 10 + 10j), (0.2 + 3j, 1.45)]:
+    for f_r in (0.1, 0.5, 0.9):
+        e1, e2, f = m1 * m1, m2 * m2, f_r**3
+        ratio = ((e2 - 1) * (e1 + 2 * e2) + f * (2 * e2 + 1) * (e1 - e2)) / (
+            (e2 + 2) * (e1 + 2 * e2) + 2 * f * (e2 - 1) * (e1 - e2)
+        )
+        a1 = layered.coefficients([f_r * x_out, x_out], [m1, m2]).a[0]
+        assert abs(a1 / (-2j / 3 * x_out**3 * ratio) - 1) < 10 * x_out**2
+print("the Rayleigh limit holds to order x² for every case")
+```
+
+**Energy.** A particle of real indices absorbs nothing, and for absorbing ones the extinction
+equals the forward amplitude (the optical theorem), computed independently:
+
+```{code-cell} python
+q = layered.efficiencies([3.0, 6.0, 9.0], [1.5, 2.5, 1.33])
+assert q.q_abs == 0 and q.q_ext == q.q_sca
+x, m = [20.0, 35.0], [1.95 + 0.79j, 1.43 + 1e-8j]
+q, s = layered.efficiencies(x, m), layered.amplitudes(x, m, 1.0)
+assert abs(4 / 35.0**2 * s.s1.real / q.q_ext - 1) < 1e-12
+```
+
+### Comparison with scattnlay
+
+[scattnlay](https://github.com/ovidiopr/scattnlay) (Peña & Pal 2009) computes layered spheres
+by the recursion scatrs builds on, in double precision. The script
+`benchmarks/compare_scattnlay.py` measures both, and the textbook method (direct matching at
+the interfaces in double precision, as Bohren & Huffman's BHCOAT does), against the 362 Arb
+reference cases inside scatrs's validated domain. The results it wrote are in
+`benchmarks/results/2026-10-14-arm64-scattnlay.json`. Apple M4 Pro, macOS; scatrs 0.2.0,
+scattnlay 2.4, 2026-10-14.
+
+| output | scatrs: largest error | scattnlay: cases above $10^{-10}$, above $10^{-6}$, failed | direct matching: the same |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| $a_n$, $b_n$ | $2.9\cdot10^{-12}$ | 118, 50, 9 | 143, 94, 43 |
+| $Q_\text{ext}$ | $4.1\cdot10^{-12}$ | 102, 63, 6 | 157, 111, 59 |
+| $Q_\text{sca}$ | $4.1\cdot10^{-12}$ | 100, 49, 6 | 151, 108, 59 |
+| $Q_\text{abs}$ | $6.3\cdot10^{-11}$ | 174, 71, 6 | 205, 110, 53 |
+| $Q_\text{back}$ | $5.4\cdot10^{-11}$ | 110, 46, 6 | 188, 109, 59 |
+| $g$ | $7.3\cdot10^{-12}$ | 108, 55, 9 | 173, 131, 59 |
+| $S_1$, $S_2$ | $9.3\cdot10^{-11}$ | 140, 50, 6 | 142, 50, 0 |
+
+The errors are measured as in {doc}`accuracy` (relative, $Q_\text{back}$ relative to the larger
+of $Q_\text{back}$ and $Q_\text{sca}$, $g$ to the larger of $|g|$ and $10^{-3}$, the amplitudes
+to the larger of $|S|$ and its RMS size); a failure is a missing or non-finite result. Where
+scattnlay disagrees, Arb sides with scatrs. Its errors have identifiable causes:
+
+- **Absorption.** scattnlay computes $Q_\text{abs} = Q_\text{ext} - Q_\text{sca}$, which loses
+  the digits of a weak absorption: a relative error of 2.4 for a core of index
+  $1.5 + 10^{-8}i$ in a clear shell at $x_L = 1$. scatrs computes $Q_\text{abs}$ directly
+  ({doc}`/theory/layered`).
+- **Very small particles.** Below $x \approx 10^{-3}$, scattnlay reduces its number of terms
+  ("nmax value was changed due to unexpected error") or fails, and its numerators lose the
+  cancelling terms that scatrs removes.
+- **Near-unity indices and air shells**: for a small core in an air shell, errors of
+  $4\cdot10^{-7}$ in the coefficients and $3\cdot10^{-4}$ in $g$.
+
+On one thread, scatrs computed the efficiencies of a coated sphere (black carbon in sulfate,
+core radius half the outer) 4.3 to 8.4 times faster:
+
+| $x_L$ | scatrs (µs) | with every derivative (µs) | scattnlay (µs) |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| 1 | 1.7 | 9.0 | 14.2 |
+| 10 | 2.7 | 18.4 | 21.1 |
+| 100 | 10.9 | 83.7 | 58.3 |
+| 1000 | 104 | | 451 |
+
 ## Size distributions
 
 For averages over size distributions, {doc}`sasktran2` compares scatrs with the Mie

## Hand-off to 3c

The 3a plan's hand-off still applies: `Particle::LayeredSphere`, its `Optics` and `Solver` arms, the real fraction directions, and the release notes. This phase adds the following.

- **Derivatives above x_L = 100.** The engine's per-node calls use `Layered::compute_with_derivatives`, which refuses x_L > 100 within the domain. An ensemble of layered spheres with derivatives must:
  - keep its size range inside that bound, or pass `allow_unvalidated`;
  - or wait for the closed-form derivatives (`docs/plans/phase-3b-followups.md`).

  Decide which before 3c's spec section 5.2 is implemented.
- **Workspaces.** The engine's `Solver` holds a `Layered<f64>`. Its dual kernels are allocated on first use, so keep one per thread, as for `Mie`.
- **The radius-fraction derivative.** ∂/∂f_l = x_L·∂/∂x_l, and ∂/∂x_L at fixed fractions is Σ_l f_l·∂/∂x_l. Both come from the per-layer groups (`LayeredSolution::group(l)`, whose `outer` flag is set for the last layer).
